NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: November 14, 2017 
Case No: 17-31 
Action : Special Use Permit 
Applicant/Owner:  Jace&Lindsay Baum Tom&Angie Caldwell   
Project Name:  Blue Rooster Ranch   
 
PARCEL INFORMATION: 
 
Address: 6210 N Old Woodruff Rd.   
  
APN: 110-09-001S & 001T 
 
Legal Desc:  Township 16 North, Range 22 East, Section 29, in the Woodruff area. 
 
District: III 
 
Area: Woodruff, AZ 
 
Parcel Size: 559.24 Acres 
 
 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  The applicant is requesting a Special Use Permit to allow 
for the use of a hunting and processing ranch with lodging accommodations for privately 
scheduled hunts. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD: The subject parcel is zoned A-General.  
Property along the road and throughout the area is consistently zoned A-General with single-
family residential homes located upon several properties.

<!-- page 554 -->
ZONING DISTRICTS:  
 
ARTICLE 3 – (A-GENERAL) GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT    
Section 301 - Purpose.  
- The main purpose of this A-General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the 
County, except land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of 
Navajo County not otherwise designated for some other specific zone, to be included in the 
“A-General Zone”, by this Ordinance. No subdividing shall be conducted or approved in the 
“A-General Zone” , without prior re-zoning of the land so intended. Uses permitted in the “A-
General Zone”, include farm and non-farm residential uses, farms, recreational, institutional, 
commercial and industrial uses as specifically listed in this Article.

<!-- page 555 -->
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan, characterizes the area 
as Rural Edge.  (Section 4.4.3 of the Comprehensive Plan.)  The character of Rural Edge is low 
to medium density residential and limited commercial uses.  Commercial uses should be 
allowed and is encouraged at Highway intersections. The proposed project fits within the 
recommendations of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan. 
 
CHANGING CONDITIONS:  The proposed use would be permitted in the A-General zoning 
district by approval of a Special Use Permit.  The hunt ranch will create a slight increase in traffic 
as the existing parcel has only a mobile home on it. 
 
 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
1. The legal notice for this item has been printed in the local  newspaper, in compliance 
with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 29 of the Zoning Ordinance.  Notices were 
sent to parcels that were within 500 feet of the subject project, instead of the 300 feet 
as required by Statutes. 
2. Notice was posted near the project site and in the Woodruff Post Office per the 
Navajo County Ordinance. 
 
3. The applicant has submitted the supporting documents required by Section 2902 (1), 
and said supporting documents are complete and adequate for the  Commission’s 
purposes. 
4. The subject property is currently zoned A-General.  
5. As of today’s date, zero (0) comments of concern have been expressed by the public.

<!-- page 556 -->
6. One Woodruff resident spoke at and attended the P&Z public hearing and stated no 
objection to the project. 
7. County Road/Snowflake Woodruff Road is a public ingress/egress easement. 
8. The Planning &  Zoning Commission recommended approval of this Special Use 
Permit request to the Board of Supervisors unanimously at the October 19 th public 
hearing. 
 
 
 
PUBLIC WORKS AND OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENT’S COMMENTS:  
 
COUNTY ATTORNEY: 
No objection, as long as the applicant complies with all rules and regulations of Navajo County, 
State of Arizona and Federal Agencies. 
 
Initial:  B.S.C. Date:  10-20-17 
 
ENGINEERING & TRAFFIC: 
Engineering staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no Engineering 
objections given that the conditions listed below are met.   
Initial: W.R.B. Date:  10-20-17 
 
 
DRAINAGE & FLOOD CONTROL: 
The Flood Control staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no objections 
given that the conditions listed below are met. 
 
Initial: B.P. Date:  10-20-17 
 
PLANNING & ZONING: 
The Planning & Zoning staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no 
objections, given that the conditions listed below are met. 
 
Initial: S.P. Date:  10-20-17 
 
 
 
PLANNING AND ZONING RECOMMENDATION:  Should the Board grant approval of this 
Special Use Permit, staff recommends the following conditions be applied: 
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall permit Blue Rooster Ranch, LLC to operate a hunting ranch  on the 
subject property as shown on the site plan dated October 2017 
 
2. This Special Use Permit is associated with the business , Blue Rooster Ranch, LLC, and shall not 
run with the land. 
 
3. This Special Use Permit is contingent upon the business being in compliance with the requirements 
of the D epartment of Agriculture, Arizona Game and Fish Department, and the Bureau of Land 
Management.  This permit will be s uspended if the business is not in compliance with Navajo 
County and the other jurisdictions requirements.  This permit could be revoked if the business is not 
brought into compliance within a timely manner.

<!-- page 557 -->
4. The permitted special use shall be allowed to occur only on the property shown on the approved site 
plan. 
 
5. Signs shall be posted on the perimeter of the property identifying the property and a contact number 
for the business and these signs shall conform to the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance. 
 
6. Property must be fenced to a height of 6’ to reduce impacts to surrounding properties. 
 
7. Perimeter protection to be maintained to contain animals. 
 
8. Security lighting shall be directed downward, shielded, preventing light trespass. 
 
9. Traffic routing signs should be clearly visible.  
 
10. The sighting-in area is for use by the hunt ranch guests only between the  sun up to sun down, no 
sooner than 7am. 
 
11. If the business is sold, the new owners shall submit operation plans to the County to demonstrate 
compliance with this permit. 
 
12. All required Federal, State, ADOT and Navajo County permits must be obtained and adhered to 
prior to operation of facility.

<!-- page 583 -->
At Blue Rooster Ranch we offer amazing hunting experiences for hunters 
from all over the United States.  The memories made at our Ranch will last a life 
time.  Like other American traditions, hunting is shared by young and old, rich and 
poor regardless of social and economic status.  Hunting knows no geographic or 
political boundaries.  Hunting provides a healthy escape steeped in heritage and 
tradition.  A tradition passed down from one generation to the next.  We believe 
our state of Arizona is losing hunting revenue to other states.  Our Arizona 
hunters are leaving here and traveling to hunting ranches all over the U.S.  These 
hunting ranches in the Western United States require a 3 month in advance 
reservation!  Jace and Tom have visited many of these hunting ranches and have 
even hunted the Duton hunting ranch in Kingman, AZ last year.  It was then we 
decided we wanted to offer this very thing in our own back yard.   
 
We all know how hard it is to draw a tag in Arizona, especially for big game.  
A very small percentage of applicants to AZ Game and Fish’s draw each year will 
be awarded a tag.  Our Ranch offers hunters a year round opportunity to hunt 
without having to be drawn for a tag and all of our hunters will have a very high 
likely-hood of success to harvest an animal. No tags, no seasons, no limits at Blue 
Rooster Ranch! 
 
Hunting is a big business.  Hunting in this country generates $25 billion in 
retail sales, $17 billion in salaries and wages, and employs 575,000 Americans, 
creating sales tax, state income tax and federal income tax revenues for 
government agencies and the people they serve.  By this past spring over 16.9 
million people had done some form of hunting in the last 12 months in the United 
States.  All hunting-related expenditures in Arizona totaled $338 million in 2011.  
Please refer to hunting expenditures in Arizona document attached.  We believe 
the future hunting traffic our Ranch will generate through Navajo County will 
drive our economies.  Stores, gas stations, restaurants, and more will make and 
sell goods and services to these hunters and in turn their employees will spend 
their earnings and a positive ripple flows through our economy.  We honestly 
hope someone in Woodruff decides to open a hamburger stand and feed all these 
hunters!

<!-- page 584 -->
The Blue Rooster Ranch is located just south of the town of Woodruff.  Our 
ranch encompasses 2 parcels.  The large land parcel is 519.24 acres and the 
smaller parcel is 40 acres.  Total acreage is 559.24 acres.  The Little Colorado River 
flows through our 2.1 mile long canyon.  We have a 2002 manufactured home 
that will serve as our “hunting lodge.”  This lodge is 2,432 square feet with 3 
bedrooms, a den and 2 bathrooms.   A 2,000 square foot Quonset hut is located 
near the lodge.  This Quonset hut will provide storage, a walk-in freezer, and 
houses the solar system batteries and well equipment.  The lodge and hut are 
powered solely by a solar power system and a private well provides water.  The 
County Rd Snowflake-Woodruff Rd runs through the east side of our ranch.  The 
fence the animals will reside in and all hunting activity will take place on the west 
side of the county road and so will not hinder or obstruct the use of this road.  We 
have plans to install a sighting-in area for our hunting customers.  This area will be 
used for target practice and to sight-in firearms.  It will be located on the east side 
of the County Rd Snowflake-Woodruff Rd near our property line. Metal gong 
targets will be placed at 100, 200, 500, 1000 yards.  We will install a berm barrier 
on our property to stop bullets from traveling south off of our property.  There is 
also a hill on the parcel to the south east of our land that will act as a natural 
barrier.  Please refer to map. Hours of operation for the sighting-in area will be 8 
am -5 pm.   
 
We met with Director Killian of the Department of Agriculture, Jeff Grant, 
Sharma Torrens, Hunter Moore, Dr. Peter Mundschenk, Leatta McLaughlin, Jill 
Metzinger, Glenn Kephart, and T. Gray with AZ Game and Fish on June 28, 2017 at 
the Department of Agriculture headquarters in Phoenix.  Arizona Game and Fish 
approved 15 different species and hybrids for our ranch.  See attached letter.  AZ 
Game and Fish Department also deferred to the Department of Agriculture 
because all of our animals are considered livestock.  Department of Agriculture 
gave their approval as long as animals from out of state have passed the right 
inspections and have traveling papers.  We have attached the state entry 
requirements for bison, goats, sheep, and swine.  We will abide by these

<!-- page 585 -->
requirements when crossing state lines with these animals.  Right now, these are 
the only animals that will be available to hunt.  As we expand and offer more 
animals, we will research and comply with the State’s entry requirements.  We 
will and are trying to source most of our animals from inside the state of Arizona.  
Only when necessary will animals will come from different states.   
 
We have installed new fencing at the ranch.  This fence is made of heavy 
duty 10 foot tall T posts and 12.5 gauge field fencing that stands at 56 inches tall.  
Our T posts are driven 2 feet into the ground.  Every 600 + feet we have installed 
H braces.  We initially got bids from local contractors to install the new fencing 
around the Ranch but we decided to tackle the project ourselves.  We have gone 
above and beyond the professional recommendations for this fence.  We are very 
proud of this fence.  Gates have been installed around the perimeter of the ranch.  
These gates will be used to unload and load animals and give hunters access to 
different parts of the ranch.  We also have made a road that follows the 
perimeter of the fence on the inside of our property.  Please refer to map, fence, 
gate and road pictures attached.   
 
We will be conducting fence checks regularly.  We will have 1-2 guides 
present at the ranch every day to help with this.  We will also maintain an 
inventory list of all the animals.  We will be proactive in making sure our animals 
are accounted for and on our land.  The guides, the fence, the fence checks, 
inventory counts, and trail cameras installed around the ranch will all help us with 
this task.  Signs will also be posted around the perimeter of our ranch that will 
state: No Tresspassing/No Hunting /Active Hunting Ranch and our phone number: 
928-241-3221.  This way citizens will know exactly who to call if a concern arises 
about our animals or any other issues.  It is in our best interest to keep these 
animals on our property.  They are our main source of income.  We lose money if 
we lose an animal.  We have purchased a liability insurance policy that covers our 
livestock through Hancock Leavitt Insurance in Taylor, AZ.  We want to be wise 
stewards of these animals and good neighbors; we will be proactive and act 
swiftly when dealing with any animal that gets out of our property.

<!-- page 586 -->
As we get approval for more species from AZ Game and Fish we will install 
additional fencing up to 8 feet tall, on top of our existing fence.  We understand 
that a taller fence will require a variance through the Board of Adjustment.  The 
species we offer to hunt right now cannot jump a 56 inch high fence.  3-4 
automatic corn/protein feeders will be placed at different locations for animals to 
receive additional feed.  Please refer to attached picture of Cabela’s automatic 55 
pound corn/protein feeders.  We also have plans to install a few blinds or tree 
stands around the property for hunters to hide, locate animals, and get precision 
shots.  These stands will be good for kids, older hunters and disabled hunters.  
Please refer to attached picture of Cabela’s pop up blind and tree stand. 
 
The booking and transaction process will go as follows:  Hunters will make 
reservation through phone, website, email, or Facebook.  We charge a reservation 
deposit of $100/hunter to reserve their hunt date(s).  Hunters can request a 
special hunt (bison, water buffalo, certain breeds of rams, ect.) and we will do our 
best to provide the special requested animals but rams and hogs will be our main 
hunts and always available at the ranch.  Hunters will not need a hunting license  
or tag.  Hunters of all ages are welcome.  After animal(s) has been harvested and 
processed our guide will total the charges and accept cash, check, credit card or 
PayPal right on sight before hunter leaves. 
 
When hunters arrive at the ranch, one of our guides will be there to assist 
and guide the hunters depending on the needs and skill set of hunters.  Guide will 
give a hunting/gun safety brief before hunt begins based on sound hunting 
practices.  A safety firearm and hunting handbook will be on site at all times and 
customers will not be able to bring or use their own horses, dogs, or ATV’s per our 
insurance policy.  We will make a valiant effort to ensure safe hunting and 
shooting procedures.  Our guide may at any time deem a hunter incompetent or 
unfit and remove his hunting privileges on our property. Hunters will be given a 
walkie-talkie radio so they can be in communication with guide(s).   Hunters will 
also sign our customer waiver before starting any sort of activity on the ranch.  
Please refer to our attached customer waiver.

<!-- page 587 -->
Most hunts will start at $500 and go up in price from there.  Size of animals 
and horns will determine the price of each animal.  Guide will inform hunters of 
animal prices before hunt begins.  If a hunter injures an animal – the hunter will 
be charged for the animal.  Processing, skinning, and taxidermy services will also 
be offered by guide and/or off site contractors. 
 
We are reaching out to our neighbors.  We are trying to make them aware 
of our plans.  We will not allow shooting within 400 yards of any occupied 
buildings, as this is State law.  We do not believe any of our neighbors have any 
“occupied” buildings within 400 yards but we will make a concerted effort to 
ensure all hunting activity remains within our property boundaries.  We will post 
T-posts within 200 yards of our north boundaries.    Please refer to map.  A map of 
ranch will be posted in the lodge.  Hunters will be educated prior to hunting, on 
the areas where shooting will be prohibited.   
 
We look forward to serving the citizens of this county and beyond.  We 
consider it to be a privilege to not only conduct business in Navajo County but to 
live and raise our families here.  We are excited for the hunts to begin!  Thank you 
for your time and assistance with this permit application.

<!-- page 588 -->
RESOLUTION ____-17 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
APPROVING A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR BLUE ROOSTER RANCH 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on September 22, 2017  by 
Blue Rooster Ranch, LLC  requesting a Special Use Permit  on the current A-General zoned 
parcel to allow for the establishing of a hunting and processing ranch with lodging 
accommodations; and  
 
WHEREAS the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 110-09-001S &001T a portion of T16N, R22E, Section 29, in the Woodruff area; and 
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on October 19, 2017; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supe rvisors after considering the testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the Special 
Use Permit , is consistent with the public health, safety and gener al welfare and should be 
approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors  hereby approves the 
Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall permit Blue Rooster Ranch, LLC to operate a hunting  
ranch on the subject property as shown on the site plan dated October 2017 
 
2. This Special Use Permit is associated with the business, Blue Rooster Ranch, LLC, and 
shall not run with the land. 
 
3. This Special Use Permit is contingent upon the business being i n compliance with the 
requirements of the Department of Agriculture, Arizona Game and Fish Department, and 
the Bureau of Land Management.  This permit will be suspended if the business is not in 
compliance with Navajo County and the other jurisdictions req uirements.  This permit 
could be revoked if the business is not brought into compliance within a timely manner. 
 
4. The permitted special use shall be allowed to occur only on the property shown on the 
approved site plan.

<!-- page 589 -->
5. Signs shall be posted on the perimet er of the property identifying the property and a 
contact number for the business and these signs shall conform to the Navajo County 
Zoning Ordinance. 
 
6. Property must be fenced to a height of 6’ to reduce impacts to surrounding properties. 
 
7. Perimeter protection to be maintained to contain animals. 
 
8. Security lighting shall be directed downward, shielded, preventing light trespass. 
 
9. Traffic routing signs should be clearly visible.  
 
10. The sighting-in area is for use by the hunt ranch guests only between sun up to sun down, 
no sooner than 7am. 
 
11. If the business is sold, the new owners shall submit operation plans to the County to 
demonstrate compliance with this permit. 
 
12. All required Federal, State, ADOT and Navajo County permits must be obtained and 
adhered to prior to operation of facility. 
 
 
 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas 
and ______ nays on this 14th  day of November 2017.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS

RESOLUTION ____-17 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING 
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR BLUE ROOSTER RANCH 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on September 22, 2017  by 
Blue Rooster Ranch, LLC requesting a Special Use Permit  on the current A -General zoned 
parcel to allow for the establishing of a hunting and processing ranch with lodging 
accommodations; and  
 
WHEREAS the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 110-09-001S &001T a portion of T16N, R22E, Section 29, in the Woodruff area; and 
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on October 19, 2017; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Super visors after considering the testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the Zone 
Change, is not consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should be denied; 
and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors  hereby deny the Zone 
Change. 
 
DENIED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas and ______ nays 
on this 14th  day of November 2017.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.d.        
Meeting Date: 11/14/2017  
Title: Zone Change Request by Florence 80
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Sandra Phillips, Assistant County Engineer/Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Sandra Phillips
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 
36-17 approving/denying a Zone Change on 14.1 acres currently zoned RU-1 to IND-1 on parcel
APN 107-23-002 a portion of Township 18 North, Range 19 East, Section 25 in the Joseph City
area.
Background:
This zone change is located adjacent to Interstate 40 and east of Joseph City.  The surrounding
area is a mix of large rural industrial, vacant land and commercial residential parcels.  The parcel
currently is only accessible from Interstate 40 as it is surrounded on the other sides by rock
slopes.  APS Cholla lake is across the interstate from this property.  The Contractor purchased this
property as part of his work for ADOT in cutting back the slopes that were sloughing rock onto
the interstate.  The Contractor's desires to park construction equipment on the site.
Recommendation: 
 This Zone Change was recommended to the Board with the stipulations listed below from the
P&Z Commission: 
Perimeter building or signage setback of two-hundred (200) feet in the IND-1 District. This
setback shall apply to all buildings, outdoor industrial uses and outdoor industrial storage.
1.
All outdoor storage, outdoor uses and/or loading dock shall be screened from view with a
minimum 6-foot tall solid wall or fence.
2.
Perimeter landscape setback of thirty (30) feet shall be provided if a perimeter screen wall
is located near the property line adjoining a residential use or residential zoning zone.
3.
All lighting on the subject parcels shall be fully shielded to minimize light pollution and
light trespass.
4.
All development of the site (new construction) shall comply with all applicable Navajo
County requirements, including those of the Zoning Ordinance, Flood Control Ordinance,
Building Safety ordinance, Federal, ADOT, State, etc. 
5.
Following this public meeting, the applicant requested that the trailer and barricades be allowed to
remain as is, with no further modifications or additions to the parcel.  Staff is recommending to
the Board the following stipulations, should the Zone Change request be granted: 
The existing semi trailer and barricades on the property are to be the only items stored on1.

<!-- page 592 -->
The existing semi trailer and barricades on the property are to be the only items stored on
the property.
1.
No modifications or additions to this parcel shall be made without prior plan submittal and
approval by Navajo County.
2.
The site shall be maintained in accordance with County Code requirements. 3.
No lighting is approved on this parcel.4.
All development of the site (new construction) shall comply with all applicable Navajo
County requirements, including those of the Zoning Ordinance, Flood Control Ordinance,
Building Safety ordinance, Federal, ADOT, State, etc. 
5.

BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: November 14, 2017 
Case No: 17-26 
Action : Zone Change 
Applicant/Owner:  FNF Construction LLC (Tressia Contreras)  
PARCEL INFORMATION: 
Address:       115 S. 48th St.    
APN:             107-23-002 
Legal Desc:  Township 18 North, Range 19 East, Section 25 in the Joseph City area. 
District:  I 
Area:  Joseph City, AZ 
Parcel Size: 14.13 Acres 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  The applicant requesting a zone change on the subject 
parcel located along the northern side of I -40 from the existing zoning of RU -1 to IND -1 (Light 
Industrial).  
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD: The surrounding area is a mix of large rural 
industrial, vacant land and commercial residential parcels.  The parcels are just outside the 
current Joseph City municipal limits. The subject parcel is between Joseph City and Holbrook.

<!-- page 594 -->
There is currently an illegally parked trailer on the property on an elevated berm. 
The zoning the parcel currently is RU-1 and the IND-1 is being proposed .

<!-- page 595 -->
ZONING DISTRICTS:  
 
ARTICLE 8 – (RURAL 1) RURAL ZONING DISTRICT ONE (1) ACRE PER DWELLING UNIT  
Section 801 - Purpose. 
- The primary purpose of this district is to conserve and protect open land uses, foster orderly growth 
in rural areas, and prevent urban agricultural land use conflicts.  The purpose of requiring large 
minimum parcels of not less than one (1) acre in area is to discourage small lot or residential 
subdivisions where public facilities such as water, sewage disposal, parks and playgrounds, and 
governmental services such as police and fire protection are not available or could not reasonably 
be made currently available. Uses permitted in this zoning district include both farm and non-farm 
residential uses, farms, and recreational and institutional uses. 
ARTICLE 18  - (IND-1) LIGHT INDUSTRIAL ZONING DISTRICT   
Section 901 - Purpose.  
- The principal purpose of this zoning distric t is to provide for light industrial uses in  locations 
which are suitable and appropriate taking into consideration the land uses on adjacent or 
nearby properties, access to a major street or highway, rail service or other means of 
transportation, and the availability of public utilities.  Principle uses permitted in this zoning 
district include the manufacture, compounding, processing, packaging or treatment of materials 
which do not cause or produce objectionable effects that would impose hazards to adjac ent or 
other properties by reason of smoke, soot, dust, radiation, odor, noises, vibrations, heat, glare, 
toxic fumes, or other conditions that would affect adversely the public health, safety and general 
welfare. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan, characterizes the area 
as Rural Ranch.  (Section 4.4.1 of the Comprehensive Plan.) 
 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
1. The legal notice for this item has been printed in the local  newspaper, in compliance 
with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 29 of the Zoning Ordinance.

<!-- page 596 -->
2. The site was posted, as well as nearby intersection, in accordance with the Statutes. 
 
3. The applicant has submitted the supporting documents required by Section 2902 (1), 
and said supporting documents are complete and ad equate for the  Commission’s 
purposes. 
4. The subject property is zoned  RU-1 and contains an illegally parked trailer visible 
from I-40. 
5. The applicant requested a zone change to IND-1. 
6. The Planning & Zoning Commission recommended approval to the Board of 
Supervisors of this zone change request 6-1 at the October 19th public hearing. 
 
PUBLIC WORKS AND OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENT’S COMMENTS:  
 
COUNTY ATTORNEY: 
No objection, as long as the applicant complies with all rules and regulations of Navajo County 
and the State of Arizona.

ENGINEERING & TRAFFIC:

given that the conditions listed below are met.

DRAINAGE & FLOOD CONTROL:

PLANNING & ZONING: 
The Planning & Zoning staff has reviewed the proposed Zone Change and has no objections, 
given that the conditions listed below are met. 
 
Initial: S.P. Date:  10-20-17 
 
PLANNING AND ZONING RECOMMENDATION:  Should the Board grant approval of the 
Zone Change, staff recommends the conditions below be applied:   
1. The existing semi trailer and barricades on the property are to be the only items stored 
on the property. 
2. No modifications or additions to this parcel shall be made without prior plan submittal and 
approval by Navajo County. 
3. The site shall be maintained in accordance with County Code requirements.   
4. No lighting is approved on this parcel. 
5. All development of the site (new construction) shall comply with all applicable Navajo 
County requirements, including those of the Zoning Ordinance, Flood Control Ordinance, 
Building Safety ordinance, Federal, ADOT, State, etc.

Attention: Sandra Phillips, P.E., CFM Asst. County Engineer/Flood Control Mgr/Planning Mgr
Reference: Parcel No. 107-23-002
Subject: Letter of Intent to Request a Zone Change
Dear Ms. Phillips,
As the owner of the referenced parcel, Florence 80, LLC is requesting a zone change on the property from RU- 
1 to Light Industrial, IND-1.
Reason for Proposed Change
The current zoning does not allow us to park our construction equipment on the site and changing the zoning 
to light industrial will allow such activity.
Benefit to the Area
The property is surrounded by Arizona Public Service lands to the east, west and south, and Forest Service 
lands are to the north. Interstate 40 (ADOT) is also along the property border to the west. Having a 
contractor yard in this area mostly surrounded by Heavy Industrial and an Interstate Highway would neither 
provide a benefit or a detriment to the area. It would be a compatible use with the surrounding activity.
Use of Property
We would like this property to be a contractor yard for parking construction equipment.
Schedule of Development
There is currently a construction trailer parked on the property. It is not anticipated, at this time, that any 
further development will take place on the property.
We hope this request and submittal meets with your approval. If you have any questions or require any 
further information please contact me at 480-929-6733 or e-mail Tressia(5)fnfinc.com
Sincerely,
FLORENCE 80, LLC.
Tressia Contreras

RESOLUTION ____-17 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
APPROVING A ZONE CHANGE FOR FLORENCE 80, LLC 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Zone change was duly filed on July 25, 2017  by Florence 80, 
LLC, requesting a Zone C hange from the RU-1 zoning classification to the Industrial 1 zoning 
classification; and  
 
WHEREAS the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN 107-23-002 which is a portion of Section 25 in T18N R19E, in the Joseph City area; and 
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on October 19, 2017; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hea ring, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the Zone 
Change, is consistent with the public health, safety and gener al welfare and should be approved 
subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of S upervisors hereby approves the Zone 
Change, subject to the following conditions: 
1. The existing semi trailer and barricades on the property are to be the only items stored 
on the property. 
2. No modifications or additions to this parcel shall be made without prior plan submittal 
and approval by Navajo County. 
3. The site shall be maintained in accordance with County Code requirements.   
4. No lighting is approved on this parcel. 
5. All development of the site (new construction) shall comply with all applicable Navajo 
County requirements, including those of the Zoning Ordinance, Flood Control 
Ordinance, Building Safety ordinance, Federal, ADOT, State, etc.  
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas 
and ______ nays on this 14th day of November 2017.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.e.        
Meeting Date: 11/14/2017  
Title: Amend Addenda & Additions Wind Load Design Criteria
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Sandra Phillips, Assistant County Engineer/Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Sandra Phillips
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 37-17
approving/denying a revision to the Navajo County Addenda & Additions to the IBC, page 4, the
Design Load Table to reflect wind speeds for signs, cell towers, cell tower modifications and
similar construction to a wind load in alignment with the current state code.
Background:
As Navajo County prepares to adopt the current IBC and IRC, Staff is recommending, to protect
public safety and welfare, that new signs, cell towers and cell tower modifications and similar
construction items be designed to withstand the increased wind loads noted in these current codes,
in the interim.  This has no affect on residential or commercial buildings. 
Green text in attachment is what has changed.
Recommendation: 
Planning & Zoning Commission and Staff recommends approval of this revision to the Navajo
County Addenda & Additions to the IBC.

RESOLUTION ____-17 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
APPROVING CLARIFICATIONS TO WIND LOAD TABLE IN THE NAVAJO 
COUNTY ADDENDUM & ADDITIONS TO THE INTERNATIONAL BUILDING CODE 
 
WHEREAS, a submittal for the revision of the Navajo Co unty Addenda & Additions to the 
International Building Code, page 4 the Design Load Table to reflect wind speeds for signs, cell 
towers, cell tower modifications and similar construction to a wind load in alignment with the 
current state code  was presented before the Planning and Zoning Commission on October 19 , 
2017;  
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on October 19 , 2017  and recommended for 
approval by a vote of 7-0; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that the 
Clarification Note, is consistent with the public health, safety and ge neral welfare and should be 
approved; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby approves the 
following clarification note at the bottom of the wind load table:  
 
All Signs, Cell Towers, and similar structures and any changes to existing Cell 
Towers to be designed for a load of 115mph, 3 second gust or current State 
Building Code. 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas 
and ______ nays on this 14th day of November 2017.
